It started right around the time the groundskeeping contract changed at work. They shifted our schedule, and the longer days just completely took over my life. I've been feeling like this ever since.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you no longer enjoy things that used to make you happy, like hobbies, time with family, or activities you used to look forward to?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still like seeing my kids, and I still don't mind working on my truck when I have the time. It's not that I don't enjoy those things anymore... it's just that I always have that heavy feeling while I'm doing them. So I still like my hobbies and my family, I just feel down all the time regardless.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy levels or do you feel unusually tired these days, even after getting enough rest?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I'm just... I'm tired all the time. By midmorning, my body already feels like I've put in a full day of work. And it doesn't seem to matter how much rest I get, really. Even after sleeping in on a Sunday, I still just drag through the simplest things. You know, just loading up my tools, checking the job plans for the week, or even just making dinner for the kids... it all just feels like it takes everything I've got. I'm just wiped out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loss of interest or pleasure in activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think we just talked about that a minute ago. Like I said, I still enjoy things. I still like seeing my kids and working on my truck. It's not that I've lost interest, it's just that I always feel down while I'm doing those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself doing things more slowly than usual, or feeling like it's hard to get motivated to do even simple tasks?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed myself moving any slower than normal, anyway. I still get out to my job sites and get the landscaping done, and I know what I have to do for the kids. The motivation is there, I guess... I just push through it. Like I was saying a minute ago, it just takes all my energy to actually do it. But no, I wouldn't say I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even beating yourself up over things that wouldn’t have bothered you as much before?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel worthless, and I'm not really beating myself up over things. I mean, I know I'm a good dad and I do good work at my job. It's just... I just feel low all the time. But it's not because I feel guilty or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating a lot more or a lot less than usual?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite's been pretty much the same. It's not that I've lost interest, it's just that I always feel down while I'm doing those things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself doing things more slowly than usual, or feeling like it's hard to get motivated to do even simple tasks?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I haven't noticed myself moving any slower than normal, anyway. I still get out to my job sites and get the landscaping done, and I know what I have to do for the kids. The motivation is there, I guess... I just push through it. Like I was saying a minute ago, it just takes all my energy to actually do it. But no, I wouldn't say I'm moving in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, maybe even beating yourself up over things that wouldn’t have bothered you as much before?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel worthless, and I'm not really beating myself up over things. I mean, I know I'm a good dad and I do good work at my job. It's just... I just feel low all the time. But it's not because I feel guilty or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating a lot more or a lot less than usual?\n\nPatient: No, my appetite's been pretty much the same. I haven't noticed any weight changes either. I still eat my regular meals, it's just... normal, I guess. Nothing's really changed there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any problems with your sleep lately, like trouble falling asleep, waking up in the middle of the night, or sleeping too much?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really had any issues there. I sleep fine, honestly. Being out on the job sites all day, doing all that physical work... by the time night rolls around, I'm pretty beat. So I don't have trouble falling asleep or anything. I just sleep my normal hours and get up when the alarm goes off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life isn't worth living, or had thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I mean, I feel down, like I said, but... I don't want to hurt myself or anything. I know life is worth living, I've got my kids and all. It's just... it's just been a real slog lately, getting through the days. But no, never any thoughts about ending it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself feeling restless, fidgety, or unable to sit still, or on the flip side, feeling like your thoughts or movements have slowed down significantly?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I'm not really the fidgety type, and I haven't noticed myself moving any slower than usual. Like I said, I'm doing physical work all day, so my body's just doing what it needs to do. I haven't felt restless or anything like that. Just... tired, but physically, everything's pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any major stress, trauma, or difficult life events in the past year that have affected you emotionally?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing major like a trauma or anything. Just the usual day-to-day stuff, you know? Work can get stressful sometimes, especially when the weather doesn't cooperate and we get behind on jobs, but... nothing out of the ordinary. Just the regular grind.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or feel less enjoyment from activities that you used to find satisfying, even if you still technically like them?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so.
